| W4-007 | B2 scoring & similarity rebuild | MUST | L | 001 | done | 29,30 | L task, SPLIT BY DESIGN across two sessions (the W4-003/004/006 precedent). **Session 29 landed the SCORING CORE half; the task stays `in_progress` for the evidence half.** Four structural defects in D18 are closed. **(1) Un-normalised totals:** mood weights summed to 0.95 and intent weights to 1.20, so the same `total` meant different things in the two modes and the flat 0.40 exposure penalty bit ~26% harder under intent purely as an arithmetic artefact; each mode now normalises to Sum w = 1 (M.9), pinned by construction rather than by assertion - a track that maxes every term scores EXACTLY 1.0 in both modes (it scored 0.95 and 1.20 before, which the kill-switch test still shows). **(2) Mixed kernels:** all four feature dims are now Gaussian with an explicit sigma, and the energy sigma is finally the band half-width instead of a constant, so a wide band forgives what a narrow one punishes - v1 used the midpoint only, so band width had literally no say in the fit (a track at 0.75 energy scored identically against a 0.1-0.9 band and a 0.45-0.55 one). A track at the band edge now lands at exp(-1/2) in ANY band. **(3) One measured dim could score 1.0:** v1 averaged over whichever dims happened to be present, so a track with nothing but a dead-on tempo claimed a perfect fit and outranked a fully measured track that was merely very good; every dim the TARGET constrains now has a place in the mean, and a dim the target does NOT constrain is excluded outright rather than counted as missing (an absent acoustic bias is a lack of preference, not a failed measurement). **(4) Provenance stored and discarded:** `featuresOf` - the ONE projection - now carries `source` + `confidence`, which AudioFeature has always recorded and the selection path threw away at exactly that line. **The formula changed shape mid-task, and the reason is worth keeping.** The first implementation followed M.9 literally as a confidence-weighted mean, `fit = Sum(w.c.k)/Sum(w.c)`. Two tests went red and were RIGHT to: with a per-doc confidence (which is what the store actually holds - confidence is one fact per row, not per dim) the `c` cancels algebraically out of that ratio, so a ReccoBeats measurement and a 0.4-confidence LLM guess produced byte-identical fits and totals. The source tiers would have been decorative, and the pin asserting them would have passed on `featureMass` alone - a reported field nothing ranked on. The literal product form `Sum w.c.k` was the other candidate and is worse for a different reason: it makes confidence a ONE-WAY discount, so a 0.3-confidence guess that a track is terrible condemns it exactly as hard as a measured fact would, while the same guess that it is perfect is heavily penalised. Shipped instead as a mass mixture, `k_eff = m.k + (1-m).0.5`: low confidence pulls toward "no opinion" in BOTH directions, and a missing dim is the same object with its evidence set to the prior. Consequences pinned: an api track beats an acousticbrainz track beats an llm track at the same fit, AND an llm track that scores badly is protected relative to a measured one that scores equally badly. Honest note on the constant: under this form `MISSING_MASS = 0.3` does not move the ranking (a missing dim lands at 0.5 whatever its mass) - it is bookkeeping that makes `featureMass` mean "how much of this fit rests on measurement", which S12 and W4-008 consume. Whether mass should ALSO break ties between two tracks at equal fit is left open deliberately: the golden-set harness in the next half is what would show whether it matters, so deciding it now would be deciding it blind. **A live serving-path defect was found by executing the consumer sweep and fixed here because W4-007 does not work without it.** `withinBand` read every feature through `Number(...)`, and `Number(null)` is 0 - a finite value outside every possible band - so a track with energy measured and tempo NOT measured was hard-dropped, while a track with no features at all passed. Knowing more about a track got it excluded, and this is the un-relaxable band, so the relaxation ladder never recovered those tracks. Measured before the fix on the real function: `withinBand({bpm:null, energy:0.7, valence:0.6}, {bpmCenter:120,...})` -> **false**, `withinBand(null-features)` -> **true**. That is not a tidy-up: the population it silently deleted - partially measured tracks - is exactly the population the mass mechanism exists to score, so leaving it would have made this task headline mechanism dead code on the serving path. The third copy of the same coercion (W4-D15 fixed `translate`; `baselineEngine` and `chronobiology` already carried the guard) is now the ONE read-side predicate `featureProvider.measured`, imported by the band, the scorer and MMR rather than copied a fourth time. A FOURTH copy was found in `embedding.buildVector` and deliberately NOT fixed here - it belongs to a different module and owner (W4-014), so it is queued as **W4-D21** with the measured evidence, per S2 "work you notice in passing goes to the backlog". **Also landed:** the shared `selection/tempo.js` octave-folded log-tempo metric (M.10) that score, MMR and - next - W4-008 and W4-014 all share instead of each carrying its own arithmetic (MMR compared raw bpm against a 130-bpm scale, the scorer used a squared-exponential over bpmWidth; they disagreed about what "close" means). Half/double time is a known beat-tracker artefact in this corpus, so 87 and 174 bpm fold to the same groove - EXCEPT against a step cadence, where footfall has no octave. The cadence case needed a fact only `translate` holds, so it publishes `cadenceLocked` as an ADDITIVE target key (the 13 legacy keys untouched, S0.2.5): true for exactly walking/running/ cycling. `activityDriven` was rejected as the predicate and the rejection is pinned - it is true for `resting` and `winding down` too, and a workout centre is a physiology/intent blend with no footfall in it. `mmr._featureSim` v2 judges all five dims (v1 ignored acousticness and danceability entirely, so a solo acoustic ballad and a club edit at the same tempo and energy came back IDENTICAL and MMR dropped one as a duplicate), weighted, renormalised over the dims the pair SHARES, coefficients summing to 1. A sibling scale bug one level up - `defaultSimilarity` blending `0.6*feat + 0.3*genre`, so two genre-less tracks cap at 0.6 similarity forever - is queued as **W4-D22** rather than folded in, because the mandate names `_featureSim` only and changing the blend moves every playlist. **S11:** the whole change ships behind ONE `WAVE4_SCORING_V2_DISABLED` flag (scorer + MMR similarity + the band null handling - one operator-visible behaviour, the W4-D15 precedent), read per call so no restart is needed. **Verified LOAD-BEARING by stub-out, not asserted:** with the flag ON, v1 and v2 disagree on the same track, the maxed track drops from 1.0 to 0.95, the octave fold disappears from similarity, and the band goes back to dropping the null-tempo track. Both v1 pathologies are pinned side by side on the two object shapes that trigger them, because they pointed in OPPOSITE directions from one line: dims ABSENT -> a fabricated perfect 1.0; the same dims present but NULL (the shape AudioFeature actually stores) -> a fabricated terrible 0.467. v2 judges the two shapes identically, because they are the same track. **Evidence:** +68 new pins across 2 new suites (`wave4.tempo.test.js` 16, `wave4.scoringV2.test.js` 52), including a 300-round seeded fuzz over hostile inputs (null/NaN/+-Infinity/blank/boolean/array/object in every feature, target and affinity slot) asserting finite, bounded [-0.8, 1] totals and no vitals in any term key, plus a 200-round symmetry/bounds fuzz on similarity. **THREE deliberate re-pins**, each because the behaviour changed on purpose: (i) `featureProvider.test.js` "projects exactly the band-relevant fields" now expects the provenance keys; (ii) `selectionUnits.test.js` "featureless tracks pay the unknown penalty" had its BODY strengthened (no count change) - in v2 that term is reported but never subtracted, so the pin now asserts the demotion it names (featureless loses to the same track measured and on-target) instead of passing on a diagnostic field; (iii) `shadow.fullSystem.test.js` exhaustive zero-knowledge allow-list on `translate()` output gains `cadenceLocked` - worth naming rather than burying, because it is an EXACT-key assertion that went red the moment the additive key appeared, which is precisely its job: it forced the new key to be argued against the zero-knowledge boundary (a boolean derived from the activity LABEL, which the same object already publishes as activityDriven/activityIntensity/tempoBand; no vital, numeric or otherwise) instead of letting it appear unexamined. Env vars `SCORE_W_UNKNOWN` / `SCORE_INTENT_W_UNKNOWN` are inert in v2 by design (subsumed). Suite **194 suites / 2818 tests green TWICE** (2817 passed + 1 todo, exit 0 both runs; baseline 192/2749, so +2 suites and +69 tests and no pre-existing test lost in the three re-pins). Both runs verified failure-free by `grep -c` over the COMPLETE captured log rather than a summary line or a tail (the W4-D15 lesson): 0 failure markers, 0 FAIL lines. Lint 0 errors, secret scan clean, zero-knowledge clean (the diff adds no log line and no DTO field). **Session 30 owes the EVIDENCE half:** the `tests/score.v2.golden.test.js` golden-set harness with before/after diffs in the PR body, the S12 `SCORING_V2_SHADOW` v1-vs-v2 divergence telemetry (rank correlation + top-k overlap) in the pipeline, and the S10 selection wall-time budget - MMR is the known latency hotspot (975ms on a 500-track pool in the shadow audit) and v2 evaluates five dims plus two log2 calls per pair, so it needs a measured number, not a reassurance. **SESSION 30 LANDED THE EVIDENCE HALF AND THE TASK IS COMPLETE.** **The golden harness** (`tests/score.v2.golden.test.js` + committed `tests/golden/score.v2.golden.json`): five literal-target personas (calm-recovery, stress-downshift, run-cadence, evening-unwind, manual-no-signal-fresh) over ONE seeded 240-track corpus built from `sim/rng` with eight feature classes chosen to be the populations this rebuild is about - fully measured at each provenance tier, PRESENT-BUT-NULL dims, single-dim tracks, and featureless. Both scorings run END TO END through the real pipeline (the flag flips band + scorer + similarity together), so the file records what a listener would actually have received. Targets are literals, not `translate` output, because a golden that called the bio engine would churn on every bio change and stop isolating scoring. Rebaseline is `GOLDEN_UPDATE=1` and ships the file diff in the PR body - a committed JSON rather than a jest snapshot precisely because `-u` is one keystroke. **The harness paid for itself on its first run: it found a THIRD instance of `Number(null) === 0`, this time on the TARGET side.** An unconstrained request (no bpmCenter, no energy window) read as a target of 0 bpm and [0, 0] energy, so v1's UN-RELAXABLE band kept 38 of 240 tracks and its twenty picks were exclusively featureless + null-tempo - it served precisely the tracks it knew nothing about, because a measured value was evidence AGAINST a phantom. v2 keeps all 240 and picks a normal mix. The core half's `measured()` already closed it; nothing here was unit-testable, because the defect only exists END TO END. Two further defects are now quantified in playlists rather than argued in the abstract: v1 gave **9 of 20** calm-recovery slots to single-dim (tempo-only) tracks against v2's 4 (the fabricated perfect fit), and v1 picked **ZERO** null-tempo tracks across all four constrained personas while v2 picks 10 (the band fix seen from the listener's side; under the phantom target the same coercion flips sign and v1 serves nothing BUT them, 7 of 20). **One asserted invariant was DROPPED after measurement, deliberately, and the reason is pinned in the file so it is not 'restored' later:** 'v2 lands more of its fit on measurement' is FALSE as a law - mean mass moves both ways (calm 0.510->0.536 and run 0.491->0.577, but stress 0.629->0.600 and unwind 0.664->0.646). That is not a regression: v2's pool CONTAINS the partially-measured tracks v1 hard-dropped, so the two means are not like-for-like. The number is committed to the golden file (visible on change) but not asserted directionally, because the direction is not a property of the scorer. The class counts are the honest instrument and they ARE asserted. **S12** (`selection/shadowCompare.js`, pure): Spearman over tie-AVERAGED ranks (scores tie constantly here - every featureless track carries the same prior - and breaking those ties by arrival order would make the statistic depend on pool order), top-k SET overlap, and magnitude deltas, because ranks and top-k fail in opposite directions and a cutover needs both (`spearman 0.95 / overlap 0.4` = broad agreement, different playlist, which one number hides). Wired into the pipeline after scoring behind `SCORING_V2_SHADOW`. Three properties pinned rather than assumed: ZERO USER IMPACT (playlist identical flag-on vs flag-off), LOAD-BEARING (spearman and overlap both < 1 on a divergent pool - an instrument that always reports agreement is worse than none), NEVER FATAL (a throwing compare is stubbed in and generation still returns its 12 tracks). The flag is an ENABLE flag and deliberately does NOT use the S11 `Boolean(env.X)` convention - `SCORING_V2_SHADOW=0` turning the diagnostic ON would be W4-D05 in reverse. `scoreTrack` gained an explicit `version` option as the seam, so the compare never mutates `process.env` mid-generation. **Honest scope, recorded because it will be misread otherwise:** the shadow compares the two SCORERS over the candidates that were served - it runs after the band and cannot see what the band dropped. The golden harness covers that gap (its `banded` counts); the two instruments are complementary by design. **S10 - the budget did its job on its first run.** v2 measured **620 ms min** on a 500-track pool, BREAKING the 600 ms collapse ceiling, at **2.18x** v1. Root-caused by stage timings rather than by guessing: MMR 558 ms vs v1's 287 ms, while scoring was 10 ms vs 3 ms. `Math.log2` was being recomputed twice per PAIR (~250k pairs at k=50) plus five type coercions on values that cannot change. Fixed by publishing `tempo.toLog2` + `tempo.foldedDistanceLog2` so the fold arithmetic stays in ONE module (a hand-rolled fold in `mmr.js` would be exactly the divergence `tempo.js` exists to prevent) and memoizing a per-features derived vector in MMR on the `_genreSets` WeakMap precedent. Result: MMR 385 ms, total 394-400 ms, ratio **1.28-1.35** over four isolated runs - inside both the 600 ms ceiling and the 1.6x ratio budget. The residual ~20% is NOT the larger pool (MMR's pair count is window-capped, so pool size barely enters); it is the irreducible price of the similarity this task mandates, five judged dims instead of three and an octave fold instead of a subtraction. The 1.6 budget is justified by observation rather than taste: the pre-hoist code measured 1.86-2.18, so it demonstrably catches the exact regression class it exists for. **The hoist was verified behaviour-preserving by construction, not by assertion:** the PRE-hoist `mmr.js`/`tempo.js` were checked back out and re-run against the POST-hoist golden file, and they matched - pick LISTS included, not just aggregates. 4 new tempo pins cover the new entry points, including a 300-pair seeded EQUIVALENCE property over both cadence modes so the cheap path can never drift from the bpm path. **Evidence:** +42 pins across 2 new suites and 1 appended (`wave4.scoringShadow` 27, `score.v2.golden` 11, `wave4.tempo` +4), **ZERO re-pins in this half**. Suite **196 suites / 2860 tests** green TWICE, exit 0, each verified failure-free by `grep -c` over the complete captured log. Lint 0 errors on every touched file (the 22 repo-wide warnings are pre-existing and elsewhere). Secret scan clean. Rows written before affinity existed count as 1, which IS the old frequency ranking, so legacy libraries re-derive exactly as before (the pre-existing pin passes untouched). **(d) The genre term was a two-rung step:** "deep house" against an allow-list of "house" scored 0.3, identically to "death metal". L task, SPLIT BY DESIGN across two sessions (the W4-003/004/006/007 precedent). **SESSION 48 LANDED HALF 1 - the PURE CORE, both models and the full S5 registration** (`57217c8`, `76e1660`). `agents/runtime/learning/feedbackLoop.js` (pure, clock-free, RNG-free): the OLS play-window slope, the M.12 biometric reward, the behavioural table, the 0.6/0.4 combination, Track-A bucket coordinates and the Track-B posterior delta. **A SIGN DEFECT IN M.12 ITSELF was found, and the deviation is implemented deliberately and pinned so it cannot be tidied back.** The appendix writes `((slope_expected - slope_observed)/sigma) * goal` with `goal = -1` for down-regulation; evaluate it on this task OWN DoD scenario ("HR falls faster than counterfactual on calm target under stress -> positive") and observed < expected makes the first factor positive, which times -1 is NEGATIVE for the exact case the DoD calls positive. The code implements `((observed - expected)/sigma) * goal` - the appendix with the subtraction transposed - and a test asserts the two orders DISAGREE IN SIGN on that scenario, so the deviation is one red test away from being reverted rather than a silent difference between doc and code. Queued as **W4-D47** for the mission text, which a session may not edit outside a reflection. **sigma_slope is derived, not tuned.** Two independent things make a slope difference unimpressive and sigma carries both, in quadrature: the OLS standard error of the slope, `MEASUREMENT_SIGMA/sqrt(Sxx)` (IMPORTED from `anomalyFilter`, not re-stated - so five samples over two minutes automatically earns a wider sigma than forty over ten, with nothing to tune), and a physiological floor of 2 bpm/min, the smallest departure from the counterfactual attributable to the music rather than to the day. The floor turned out to need its OWN pin: the obvious "a noisy window earns less" test does NOT catch its removal (both sides simply saturate at 1), so a separate pin asserts that a precisely-measured 0.2 bpm/min divergence over a long clean window scores < 0.25 - without the floor it scores a maximal 1.0 and the whole reward collapses into a sign bit. **`models/RewardEvent.js` ships BOTH collections ADR-0012 requires kept apart:** `RewardEvent` (Track A - per-user `{stateDomain, targetBand, hourBin}` aggregates, enums taken from the taxonomy itself so a bucket can never name a state the state model does not have) and `TrackPosterior` (Track B - GLOBAL, no userId, CC0 `mbid:` only). **The Track-B gate is a query PRE-HOOK, not only a path validator, and that is the load-bearing decision here.** ADR-0012 asks for a "schema-level validator (fail-closed)", but a Mongoose `validate` does not run on `findOneAndUpdate`/`updateOne`/`updateMany` unless the CALLER passes `runValidators: true` - and an upsert is exactly how a feedback loop writes, so a validator alone is a convention a caller disarms by forgetting an option (the same trap 0.2.2 already names for encryption setters). Five pins drive real non-CC0 writes through every update verb WITHOUT that option, including a key smuggled into the `$set` rather than the filter, and a filterless upsert (refused: an insert that identifies no recording cannot be gated). **The Beta prior forced a design decision worth recording:** neither `default: 1` nor `$setOnInsert` can seed it under an `$inc` upsert - Mongoose SKIPS defaults for a path the update already touches (so a first-ever success would insert Beta(1,0), i.e. CERTAINTY, and every Thompson draw in W4-013 would come back ~1.0), and MongoDB refuses two operators on one path. `repositories/rewardRepo.js` therefore writes Track B as an aggregation-pipeline update (`$ifNull(alpha, PRIOR.alpha) + delta`), atomic and correct on both insert and update, with the prior pinned by test. **S5 registered in the same commit, all five surfaces:** `erasure.js`, `gdpr-delete.js`, `userDataExport.js`, `wearableErasure.js`, and PRIVACY_DECLARATIONS.md Retention table + Play deletion-cascade list (both stale line-range citations in that doc refreshed while there). **The per-wearable surface is a reasoned EXCLUSION rather than a copy of MorningState rule**, written INTO `wearableErasure.js` so it is registered rather than merely absent: a bucket is not wearable-derived the way a nightly vitals consolidation is - the taxonomy resolves a state DEGRADED from mood taps and the clock alone (its own `degraded` flag is exactly that guarantee) and half the evidence is behavioural, so erasing a user whole learned personalization because they unpaired one watch would be over-erasure, not caution. Retention is 365 days on `updatedAt`, deliberately unlike this repo 90-day sample convention and argued in the model header: keyed to `updatedAt` it is a STALENESS eviction (an active bucket never ages out), and 90 days would not minimize data so much as guarantee that a Saturday-morning runner ~13 observations never outrun any prior. **The pre-existing hardcoded erasure/export lists W4-012 warned about did all have to be extended by hand** (`userDataExport.test.js`, `accountDeletion.test.js`, `shadow.auth.test.js`) - none of them is mechanically guarded, and this is now the second consecutive task to pay that tax. **Evidence:** +64 new pins across 2 new suites (`feedbackLoop.test.js` 42, `rewardEvent.integration.test.js` 22), **ZERO test-count re-pins** (the three list tests gained assertions inside existing `it()` bodies). Continued the in_progress task rather than starting a new one (WAVE4_MODEL_TIER=exec, reflection NOT-DUE at 0.96h, origin/main unmoved at 44fd951, tree dirty only on `mobile/src/health/config.ts` - left uncommitted per the standing session-30 ruling). **THE ONE DECISION THAT SHAPED EVERYTHING ELSE: the judgement runs on the SOCKET side and only its RESULT crosses the queue.** The obvious build - enqueue the play window, let the worker do the maths - puts a minute-resolution heart-rate trace into a BullMQ job payload, which is an unencrypted Redis blob, and 0.2.2 bars numeric vitals from Redis in as many words. So `evaluatePlay` runs where the readings already legitimately are (`handleBiometricReading` receives them raw) and what travels is a bucket address, one bounded scalar and a CC0-only Beta delta. The payload key set is pinned CLOSED (`REWARD_JOB_KEYS`) with a serialization scan for `samples`/`heartRate`/`expectedSlope`/`sigma`/`trend`, because a field added carelessly later is exactly how a vital gets back in. **`agents/runtime/learning/playbackEvent.js` (S7, pure):** closed type enum, `positionMs` bounded to 4h and floored, `trackKey` bounded to 256 chars against a conservative charset, payload refused WHOLE past 12 keys, and unknown fields dropped by RECONSTRUCTION rather than by an allowlist somebody has to remember to update. Two refusals are deliberate and pinned as such: `type` is read as an OWN property so `{type: "toString"}` cannot resolve through the prototype, and a `spotify:` key is ADMITTED here - shape is this layer job, ADR-0012 is the model job, and a third copy of that rule furthest from the write would be a third thing to keep in sync. The per-socket budget (20/min, sliding) does NOT record a refused arrival: the naive limiter rolls its window forward on every attempt, so a client with a buggy retry loop stays locked out for as long as it keeps trying - pinned with a 500-attempt flood that recovers exactly one window after the last ADMITTED event. **`agents/runtime/learning/playWindow.js` (pure, clock-free):** the three inputs the engine cannot invent. A serve opens the window under the context that CHOSE the mix (state, band, the listener own hour, arc) rather than whatever those are when the skip finally arrives; a terminal event closes it and starts the next track at the same instant. `save` is deliberately NOT terminal - a saved track keeps playing, and if it closed the window then `behavioralReward` "completed AND saved is a maximal positive", which it dedupes by type specifically to express, could never occur. `positionMs` ANCHORS the sample window at `now - positionMs` (floored at the serve), which self-corrects the case the client never reported the PREVIOUS track at all - without it those readings get scored against this track. The counterfactual is the trend the filter held going INTO the window, with the first in-window sample only as fallback. Samples are capped at 300 and pruned past 20 minutes (S10), and a CLOSED window collects nothing at all. **The samples are the RAW device values, not the filtered level, and that is load-bearing:** the engine sizes sigma_slope from the OLS standard error `sigma_meas/sqrt(Sxx)`, which is only the right scale for INDEPENDENT observations carrying the wrist noise that figure describes - a Kalman-smoothed series has had exactly that noise removed, so scoring it against sigma_meas reads a smoothed line as an implausibly precise measurement and saturates every reward. Mutation-verified (swapping in `effectiveHR` turns one pin red). The filter still supplies the two things only it knows: whether the reading is trustworthy, and the trend at track start. **One additive targets key was needed and is argued rather than assumed:** `buildTargets` now publishes `hourOfDay`. The socket has no access to the listener habitual tz offset, so a bucket hour resolved downstream would be the SERVER hour - D13 through the back door, filing an Auckland evening as a Frankfurt one. `resolveHourContext` already computes it there; publishing it means the wind-down and the learner cannot disagree about what time it is. A clock reading is not a vital. **One DELIBERATE RE-PIN:** `wave4.affectSeam.test.js`'s "a user with NO tz data keeps today server-hour behaviour exactly" compared `buildTargets` output to `translate()` output by exact equality, so it failed on the new key rather than on the behaviour it guards; re-pinned as `{...expected, hourOfDay: serverHour}`, which is strictly STRONGER (every translate-owned key still byte-identical AND the published hour proven to be the server one on that path). That is not exploration, it is noise wearing exploration's clothes: the draw carries no information because the posterior carries none, and the cost is a real playlist reshuffled by a coin flip. `planNovelty` therefore returns a NULL budget until the bucket has at least one observation, and the pipeline reads null as impose-no-quota - so the DORMANCY INVARIANT is a property of the MATHS, not of the flag (W4-011's no-op-rather-than-a-zero precedent). It is pinned twice: flag-off, and flag-ON-with-a-Beta(2,2)-posterior, both byte-identical to the pre-W4-013 playlist. **Storage is a sub-document on the EXISTING W4-011 bucket row**, not a new collection: same address, same unique index, same TTL, same S5 registration - a second collection keyed by the same three coordinates would be the D11/W4-D42 second-disagreeing-table class and would need five erasure surfaces re-registered to hold the counterpart of a row that already exists. **S5 therefore needs NO new registration** (verified surface by surface: `erasure.js` is `deleteMany({userId})`, `userDataExport` projects the whole doc, `gdpr-delete.js` and `wearableErasure` already list RewardEvent) - and W4-011's CLOSED-key pin passes UNCHANGED, because `default: undefined` keeps the field absent until it is earned. `recordNoveltyOutcome` seeds the prior by aggregation-pipeline `$ifNull` for exactly the reason `recordTrackOutcome`'s header documents - an `$inc` against a schema default lands Beta(1,0) on insert, and every Thompson draw off that row then returns ~1.0. **The write lane needed one fact W4-011 deliberately never captured:** was the track a gamble. Captured at SERVE time (`playWindow.discoveryKeysOf`), because by the time a `playback_event` names a track nothing remembers whether the system chose it. `wasDiscovery` is a TRI-state and the third state is load-bearing: today's shipped client forwards `track_skipped` with `trackKey: null`, so the role is genuinely unknown, and reading unknown as false would let silence vote forever on a question it never answered. **Consequence worth Daniel's eye: the bandit cannot LEARN in production until the mobile app emits `playback_event` with a `trackKey`** - queued as W4-D50 and an on-device checklist item for W4-015. It is not a defect in this task (W4-011 shipped with the same property by design, per S3's own mobile-emission-to-checklist note), but it is why B5 ships genuinely dark rather than merely flagged. **Read lane:** pipeline stage 4c, a CEILING over the existing ranking (never a floor, never a re-ranking), applied BEFORE MMR so the freed slots refill with the next-best familiar tracks - trimming after MMR would serve k-n tracks. Discovery candidates are cut by the score they just earned, so a quota of 3 keeps the three BEST gambles rather than the first three seen. The serve-time role table is transient socket memory (bounded at 200 keys), never persisted, never in Redis, and the reward job payload key set stays pinned CLOSED. **Owed next session (B7):** `models/PersonalWeights.js` + `agents/runtime/learning/personalization.js` - the M.15 bounded trust-region overlay on `score._resolveWeights()`, the seam score.js's own ADR-0011 header reserves; shrink-to-global weekly; cold start = global weights; FULL S5 registration on all five surfaces (S5 names `PersonalWeights` explicitly, and unlike this half it IS a new collection); and its own dormancy invariant. **SESSION 51 LANDED B7's ENGINE, STORAGE AND READ LANE; THE WRITE LANE IS OWED AND THE SESSION WAS HALTED MID-FLIGHT - see H12.** Continued rather than started, per S2 step 5 (an `in_progress` task you own outranks a new pick); reflection NOT-DUE (2.52h), origin/main unmoved at 44fd951. `agents/runtime/learning/personalization.js` (pure, clock-free per S9). **THE LOAD-BEARING DERIVATION: M.15 never says what the gradient IS, and the naive reading is provably useless here.** Read as the partial derivative of the score w.r.t. w_d it is just the term value k_d - and every k_d is in [0,1], so a positive reward pushes all five weights up together and the renormalisation that keeps Sum w = 1 divides the whole push straight back out. A gradient that cannot change a ranking is not a gradient. The derivative of the NORMALISED score is `(k_d - p)/Sum w`, so with the M.9 invariant the gradient is `k_d - p` EXACTLY: the term value CENTRED on the preference score it helped produce. It is a pure re-allocation (`Sum w_d*grad_d = 0`, pinned by construction) and it needs no candidate-pool statistics - one served track plus the weights that served it is the complete input. **SECOND DESIGN CORRECTION, found by a test that failed and turned out to be right:** applying M.15's clamp component-wise and THEN renormalising does not deliver the bound M.15 states. Measured on the real mood weights: delta_taste=+0.4 with delta_feature=-0.4 gives Sum = 1.02, so `feature` lands at 0.588*w - outside the promised [0.6, 1.4] - and the worst case admits [0.6/1.4, 1.4/0.6], four times the intended window. The cause is that delta's component along the all-ones direction is pure GAUGE (renormalisation deletes it), so clamping it clamps a quantity with no effect while the part that matters escapes. `overlay` therefore CENTRES delta on the w-weighted mean and bounds the remainder by SCALING to radius 0.4 rather than clipping per component - scaling preserves w-orthogonality (a clip would re-introduce the gauge), so the renormalisation becomes an identity and `w_user/w_global` is inside [0.6, 1.4] EXACTLY. It is also what trust region means in the optimisation literature the constant comes from: keep the direction, bound the radius. Pinned by a 20k-case fuzz over random weight tables, with the extremes accumulated in plain JS and asserted ONCE - a per-component `expect` cost 30 s of suite time to check four numbers, the W4-D30 lesson applied. **THIRD: the trust region binds on delta, not only on w.** M.15 states the bound on the output; enforcing it only there leaves the accumulator unbounded behind a saturating output, which is textbook integrator windup - 500 positive rewards walk delta to +10 and the listener's first disagreement then moves nothing for 500 more. Clamping delta to +/-0.4 is algebraically the SAME window and keeps the state one step from the boundary. **FOURTH: the weekly shrink is a READ-TIME computation, not a job.** M.15 asks for a weekly 0.98 shrink; a repeatable job would have been the third in this repo to walk a user population, after the one W4-D44 caught consolidating the same first 500 users by `_id` every night. The stored delta is the value as of `updatedAt` and every reader multiplies by `0.98^(elapsed/week)`: same rate, same fixed point, no cliff at the week boundary, no population walk, no write on the serving path, and a missed run cannot skip a decay because there are no runs. The writer applies the identical factor before adding its step, so the stored value and every reader's view stay consistent. **Storage IS a new collection** (unlike B5's sub-document): its address is the user alone, it carries no bucket coordinates and it is read once per GENERATION rather than once per play, so folding it into `RewardEvent` would mean duplicating four numbers across every bucket a listener has, with no rule for which copy wins. `models/PersonalWeights.js` + `repositories/personalWeightsRepo.js`: the write is an aggregation PIPELINE update doing decay-then-step-then-clamp atomically, because split across a read and a write two concurrent plays each decay from the same stale stamp and one update is silently lost - on a row a single playlist can touch fifty times. The clamp lives IN the pipeline (`$min`/`$max`) because a Mongoose validator on a pipeline-written path never runs at all, the same trap S0.2.2 names for encryption setters; the schema header says so rather than pretending otherwise. **FULL S5 registration on all five surfaces** (`erasure.js`, `gdpr-delete.js`, `userDataExport.js`, the retention table in `docs/PRIVACY_DECLARATIONS.md`, and `wearableErasure.js` as a DELIBERATE EXCLUSION carrying RewardEvent's reasoning: half the evidence behind the overlay is behavioural and a scoring weight has no `source` field to scope a delete by). The stale `erasure.js:36-61` citation in the privacy doc was corrected to `:37-68` in the same edit. S5 is also EXERCISED and not only grepped: the real cascade and the real export are run against a real row, because a source-grep guard cannot catch a `deleteMany` with a typo'd filter. **Read lane:** `score.scoreTrack({weights})` - the seam score.js's own ADR-0011 header reserved - plus an exported `resolveWeights()` so the pipeline can lay the overlay over the right table ONCE per generation instead of once per candidate. v2 ONLY: `WAVE4_SCORING_V2_DISABLED` promises the whole pre-W4-007 behaviour back, and M.15's re-allocation is not even defined over the v1 tables (they do not sum to 1). **The DORMANCY INVARIANT is exact, not approximate:** `overlay` returns the caller's OWN object by IDENTITY when there is nothing to apply, so a decayed-to-nothing row cannot differ from global weights in the last few bits and flip two tied tracks. Pinned three ways - flag off, flag on with no row, flag on with a row decayed below `NEGLIGIBLE_DELTA` - plus a fourth pin that a throwing read leaves the playlist untouched. Every abstention is NAMED (`cold-start` / `no-evidence` / `no-user` / `error`), the B5 posture. **S11:** `WAVE4_PERSONAL_WEIGHTS` is an opt-IN flag, sign inverted like B5's, because S3 says STRETCH work ships dark. **Evidence: suite 216 suites / 3355 tests green TWICE** (baseline 213/3290: +3 suites, +65 tests = exactly the new pin count). Lint 0 errors / 22 warnings, unchanged. Secret + attribution + zero-knowledge scans of the diff: clean (the only `sk-` hit is the word ri-sk-register in this file's own prose). No `biometricHandler` edit this session, so risk-register #1's isolation run does not apply. **Compliance (ADR-0012): squarely Track A** - the artifact is four music-RANKING coefficients naming no recording, no provider and no catalogue, so it is not derived functionality over Spotify Content in any sense ADR-0011 bars; the tripwire and both closed-key pins pass untouched. **THREE deliberate re-pins, all additive:** `shadow.auth`, `accountDeletion` and `userDataExport` each hand-mock every model and gained a `PersonalWeights` double plus the matching erasure/export assertion - the FIRST full run of this session went RED on the latter two precisely because an un-mocked new model hangs their query, a gap the S5 source-grep guards structurally cannot see. **OWED NEXT SESSION (the B7 write lane, and it is the ONLY thing left on this row):** `selectPlaylist` now RETURNS a bounded per-served-track gradient list (`{key, g}`, keyed exactly as `playWindow.discoveryKeysOf` keys its roles) and **nothing consumes it yet** - stated plainly rather than left to be discovered. The remaining chain is the B5 write lane step for step: thread `gradients` through `generation/orchestrator.js` into `builtPlaylist`, capture it into the serve-time playWindow context beside `discoveryKeys` in `biometricHandler.recordServeSideEffects`, add a `playWindow` gradient lookup as a TRI-state (unknown must not read as zero - the `wasDiscovery` lesson), carry `personalization.stepFrom(...)` on the reward job as a `weightStep` key (a deliberate re-pin of `REWARD_JOB_KEYS`, argued as strictly less sensitive than the HR-slope-derived `reward` already on it), and call `personalWeightsRepo.applyUpdate` from `rewardIngest.worker.js` as a fourth independent write. Until that lands, B7 is a read lane with no writer, so no row is ever created and the overlay is dormant by construction as well as by flag. **SESSION 53 LANDED THAT WRITE LANE, so the row is DONE.** The chain is B5 step for step, because it carries the same shaped fact - something only the SERVE knows. `orchestrator.generateV2` threads the pipeline `gradients` capture out beside `telemetry`; `recordServeSideEffects` puts `gradientsOf(builtPlaylist.gradients)` into the play-window context next to B5 `discoveryKeys`; `playWindow.gradientsOf` turns the bounded `{key, g}` list into a Map (`MAX_GRADIENT_KEYS` 200, its OWN constant rather than an alias of B5 bound) and drops any entry whose gradient is not four finite numbers WHOLE rather than repairing it, so a malformed capture reads back as UNKNOWN instead of letting a defaulted zero pose as a measurement (the W4-D15/W4-D21 coercion class); `noteEvent` reports `gradient` as a TRI-state with the `wasDiscovery` rule verbatim - a zero gradient is a real answer, "the serve never told me" is not an answer at all. **THE ONE DESIGN CALL THAT IS NOT B5 COPIED: the step is derived from `verdict.components.combined`, NOT from `verdict.reward`.** `evaluatePlay` forces `reward` to exactly 0 for a play it cannot file under a bucket, and that is a TRACK-A rule - the bucket store is addressed by (stateDomain, targetBand, hourBin), so a reward with no bucket has nowhere to go. The overlay is addressed by the USER and carries no bucket coordinates at all, so the rule does not apply to it, and reading `reward` here would have meant "listeners whose taxonomy state cannot be resolved never learn" - precisely the degraded-signal population most in need of a ranking that adapts. `combined` is the same verdict BEFORE Track A addressability is applied and is bounded to [-1,1] by `combineReward` exactly as `reward` is. The dispatch guard widened to admit a job that teaches ONLY the overlay; it stays a guard, because `stepFrom` returns null rather than a zero step when there is no verdict or no gradient. `weightStep` is a deliberate re-pin of the CLOSED `REWARD_JOB_KEYS`: four music-RANKING coefficients each bounded by the learning rate, naming no recording, no provider and no vital - strictly less sensitive than the HR-slope-derived `reward` the payload already carries, which is the bar S0.2.2 sets for anything crossing Redis. `rewardIngest.worker` calls `personalWeightsRepo.applyUpdate` as a FOURTH independent write, and it is the only one of the four a play can produce when the taxonomy could not name a state. **THE CLOSED LOOP IS NOW PINNED, which reflection #10 named as the test the per-piece pins cannot replace:** `tests/personalWeights.convergence.test.js` runs resolveWeights -> overlay -> gradientOf -> stepFrom -> applyUpdate -> readWeights -> effectiveDeltas -> overlay against real Mongo, driven by a synthetic listener who finishes what fits the target and skips what is merely familiar (the two term vectors are ANTI-CORRELATED, so a pure re-allocation can only move by trading). It ends with more weight on `feature` and less on `taste`, and four counter-pins keep that from being vacuous: the total over SCORING_TERMS is preserved (a re-allocation, not a louder scorer), 300 consecutive agreements stay inside the trust region (windup, closed through the real pipeline write), an INDIFFERENT listener moves nothing, and a listener who stops listening drifts back. **Three of my own first assertions were WRONG and the code was right** - recorded because the corrections are the interesting part: (1) the personal-four subtotal is NOT invariant, because `discovery` carries no delta of its own but IS one of the terms the re-allocation is funded from (overlay own header says so); the invariant is SM.9 Sum over SCORING_TERMS = 1. (2) `rotation` weighs exactly 0 in mood mode, so a ratio pin over it is 0/0; the meaningful pin is that learning can never REVIVE a term the mode switched off, and that is what it asserts now. (3) "shrink-to-global" is ASYMPTOTIC: 0.98/week is x0.35 after a YEAR, not forgetting - the identity pin now derives its horizon from the constants (log(NEGLIGIBLE_DELTA/DELTA_LIMIT)/log(SHRINK_PER_WEEK), ~12 years) and a separate line pins the honest one-year figure so nobody reads the phrase as "forgets within a season". **Evidence: suite 218 suites / 3390 tests green TWICE**, exit 0 (baseline 216/3355: +2 suites, +35 tests = EXACTLY the new pin count - 27 wiring + 6 convergence + 2 orchestrator - so no pre-existing test was lost). Lint 0 errors / 22 warnings, unchanged. Secret + attribution + zero-knowledge scans of the diff: clean, and the diff adds NO console line at all. `biometricHandler.pipeline.test.js` run in ISOLATION after the handler edit per risk-register #1: 169/169. **7 mutants applied, 7 caught:** unknown-gradient-reads-as-zero, dispatch-reads-`verdict.reward`, worker-never-applies-the-step, handler-drops-the-capture, orchestrator-drops-`gradients`, `gradientsOf`-admits-a-non-finite-term, and step-applied-without-the-reward-sign. **FOUR deliberate re-pins, all additive:** the play contract gains `gradient` (pinned exhaustively in BOTH wiring suites, so a new key on a play still has to be argued), and the worker return shape gains `weights: false` in three places. **Honest coverage statement:** the pipeline->orchestrator hop has a REAL test and the window side has real tests, but the ONE line between them in `recordServeSideEffects` is pinned by SOURCE GREP, not by booting a socket - stated in the test itself rather than left to be discovered, and it is the same standard B5 `discoveryKeys` capture is held to one line above it. **Compliance (ADR-0012): Track A** - unchanged from the read lane, the artifact is four music-ranking coefficients naming no recording, no provider and no catalogue. | W4-014 | B3 embedding v2 dual-write | STRETCH | L | 007,W16 | done | 54,55 | Picked as the FIRST pending §3 row with deps done: 007 is done and W16 was RESOLVED by the W4-000 review, the MUST tier (000-008, 016) is complete, W4-015 always runs last, no pending `class: repair` row exists, and reflection was NOT-DUE (0.44h old, stamped by session 53). Tier `exec`, so the §1 saver preference against starting a new L task does not apply. L task, SPLIT BY DESIGN across two sessions (the W4-003/004/006/007/011 precedent): **session 54 owns the PURE CORE half** (`idfStats` + `buildVectorV2` + ADR-0014 + pins); the WIRING half (TrackEmbedding `vectorV2` field, adapter index/path parametrisation, `EMBEDDING_V2_WRITE` dual-write in `embedding.worker.js`, backfill script, `EMBEDDING_V2_READ` read cutover, the Atlas HITL tutorial and the v2 runbook check) is owed. **SESSION 54 LANDED THE PURE CORE; THE WIRING HALF IS OWED.** `services/vector/idfStats.js` + `buildVectorV2` in `services/vector/embedding.js` (v1 untouched, byte-identical), `docs/adr/0014-embedding-v2.md`, and `tests/wave4.embeddingV2.test.js` (39 pins). **THE LOAD-BEARING CORRECTION, and it came from a pin I wrote wrong rather than from reading:** composing `[0.8*audio ; 0.6*genre]` and stopping there leaves a GENRE-LESS track at norm 0.8, so the raw dot between two genre-less tracks is scaled by 0.64 - NOT the mission's stated "genre-less cosine unchanged vs audio-only" - and, worse, a genre-less track (norm 0.8) and a tagged one (norm 1.0) get incomparable scores. In a corpus that is ~98% genre-less that would let ANNOTATION COVERAGE MASQUERADE AS RELEVANCE, the same class of defect as D17's cross-provider affinity collision. `buildVectorV2` therefore renormalises the composed vector to unit length: a no-op by construction when both blocks are present (0.8^2 + 0.6^2 = 1, so the composition weights are never touched), load-bearing only in exactly the case the mission calls out. Pinned as an EXACT equality against the bare audio cosine, plus a pin that a genre-less and a tagged track with identical audio score exactly 0.8. **SECOND CORRECTION, also found by a failing pin: a MISSING df table is not evidence of rarity.** A genre absent from a table that EXISTS earns the rarity cap (the corpus has genuinely never seen it); a table that could not be read yields weight 0 for everything, because inferring maximal rarity from a table that was never loaded turns a load failure into a confident genre direction. **CENTRED DIMS ARE FORCED, NOT A STYLE CHOICE** - argued in the ADR rather than slipped in: sin/cos are already centred and their no-evidence point is the circle centre (0,0), so a block mixing centred and uncentred dims would have two different zeros and its L2 normalisation would mean nothing. One zero is also what lets an unmeasured dim ABSTAIN at the origin instead of masquerading as a measurement - the W4-D21 class, which v1 still carries in `fin()`. It additionally fixes a PROD-MEASURED pathology the required reading documents: v1 vectors live in the all-positive orthant where every pair scores ~0.99 and `DISCOVERY_MIN_COSINE` is nearly inert. **CONSEQUENCE, recorded as a hard prerequisite of the read cutover and not as a follow-up: `DISCOVERY_MIN_COSINE` (0.3, retuned in PR #135 against v1's compressed scale) MUST be re-tuned before `EMBEDDING_V2_READ` is switched on** - see H13. **COMPLIANCE (ADR-0011/0012), the one genuinely new surface here:** the IDF table is a PERSISTED, CROSS-USER artefact describing a catalogue, so it is fitted over the `mbid:` slice ONLY - the same boundary `embedding.worker.js` already draws for the vectors themselves, not one row wider - and the containment is enforced in the COUNTING LOOP, not only in the Mongo query, because `rows` is an injection point and a boundary a caller can step around is not a boundary (the `_dropRestricted` / RewardEvent `mbid:`-validator posture). Statistics agree with compliance here: an IDF must describe the population it is applied to, and the v2 index holds the `mbid:` slice and nothing else. **N counts GENRE-CARRYING documents, not all of them** - with N = the whole corpus, N/df is enormous for every genre, every weight pins to the cap and the IDF stops discriminating, which is exactly the failure the mission's W16 note warns about. **FAIL-SOFT throughout:** no stats -> every weight 0 -> genre block exactly zero -> v2 degrades to a correct audio-only vector; `idfStats` never throws into the embedding path. **Evidence: suite 219 suites / 3429 tests green TWICE, exit 0** (baseline 218/3390: +1 suite, +39 tests = EXACTLY the new pin count, so no pre-existing test was lost). Lint 0 errors / 22 warnings, unchanged. Secret + attribution scans of the diff clean (the only `sk-` hit is the word ri-sk-register in this file's own prose). Zero-knowledge: not applicable in substance - this task touches no vitals at all - and the diff adds exactly one console line, a corpus-load warning carrying an error message and no data. **10 mutants applied, 10 caught - and the pass paid for itself: M8 (delete the genre dedupe) SURVIVED**, because with a single genre the block L2-normalisation divides any duplication straight back out; duplication can only bend the block's DIRECTION once a second genre exists for it to out-vote. The pin was rebuilt on `['house','techno']` and now catches it, with the reason written into the test. **ZERO re-pins:** v1 is additive-only, so all 8 existing embedding-consumer suites (124 tests) pass untouched. **OWED NEXT SESSION (the wiring half), stated plainly rather than left to be discovered:** `TrackEmbedding` gains a `vectorV2`/`dimV2`/`modelV2` path (a second path on the SAME doc, so `recordingKey` stays unique and the read cutover is an index+path switch); `mongoAtlasVectorAdapter` parametrises the hardcoded `path: 'vector'` and index name; `embedding.worker.js` dual-writes behind `EMBEDDING_V2_WRITE` (and its two gate suites mock the embedding module with `buildVector` ONLY, so they need the v2 export added); a backfill script that computes the IDF table ONCE via `idfStats.use()` and hands the same blob to every vector; the `EMBEDDING_V2_READ` cutover with the `DISCOVERY_MIN_COSINE` recalibration above it; and a v2 arm on `scripts/verifyProdRunbooks` (`EXPECTED_DIM` is currently pinned to 70 in two suites). Nothing serves v2 until all of that lands AND Daniel builds the index - the core is genuinely dark, not merely flagged. **CI checked at close-out per the W4-D25 gate, not assumed: PR #180 ALL 10 CHECKS GREEN on this session's own pushed head `9cac833`** (run 32536777553) - `Backend - lint & test` 2m23s, `Mobile - jest` 1m58s, `Mobile - Android compile check` 8m37s, gitleaks, GitGuardian, frontend typecheck and both Vercel deployments. **SESSION 55 IN PROGRESS - the WIRING half.** Continuing the in_progress row this owner holds (S2 step 5). Scope owed and being executed: TrackEmbedding vectorV2/dimV2/modelV2 path, adapter index+path parametrisation, EMBEDDING_V2_WRITE dual-write in embedding.worker.js, the v2 backfill, the EMBEDDING_V2_READ cutover resolved in ONE place so the query vector and the index can never disagree, and the v2 runbook arm. **SESSION 55 LANDED THE WIRING HALF - W4-014 IS COMPLETE.** Continued the in_progress row this owner holds (S2 step 5: an in_progress task outranks a new pick); reflection NOT-DUE (1.09h old, stamped by session 53), origin/main unmoved at 44fd951, branch in sync with origin, no pending `class: repair` row. S2: the tree carried the two standing dirty files - `mobile/src/health/config.ts` (Daniel's local deployment config, left in place per the session-30 ruling) and an untracked `docs/CLAUDE_DESIGN_PROMPT.md` (Daniel's own design brief, out of Wave-4 scope) - neither committed, neither dropped. **THE DECISION THE REST HANGS OFF, and it is the one thing session 54's owed-list did not name: ONE resolver owns which space is live.** A vector search compares two things that must live in the same space - the query vector we build, and the index+path we search it against - and those were two independent decisions in two modules (`discovery/targetVector.js` and `mongoAtlasVectorAdapter.js`). When they disagree the failure is the worst kind available here: dims differ (70 vs 135) -> `$vectorSearch` throws -> the adapter's catch degrades to `[]` -> **discovery is silently OFF with no error anywhere**; dims coincide (a future v3 sized like v1) -> no failure at all, just a coordinate-wise comparison of two unrelated geometries returning confident nonsense. So neither module reads the flag. `services/vector/embeddingSpace.js` holds one frozen record per space binding the four things that must move together (path, dim, model tag, index-name env); `discoveryVectorService.find()` resolves it ONCE PER CALL and hands the same value to the query builder and to `queryNear`; `pipeline`'s MMR embedding load reads the same resolver, so one generation can never retrieve in one space while judging similarity in another. Every read-path pin asserts the PAIR, never one side of it. Reading v2 while NOT writing it is honoured (an operator may be mid-cutover with a finished backfill) but warned ONCE - it is the single misconfiguration that looks like success: new tracks stop entering the index, which goes stale with no symptom except slowly thinner discovery. **THE FINDING THIS HALF PRODUCED, measured rather than reasoned about afterwards, and it is a NEW hard prerequisite of the cutover: annotation MATCH is worth exactly 0.8 in either direction.** A v2 genre block is 0.6 of a unit vector when a track is tagged and EXACTLY ZERO when it is not, so query and candidate must AGREE about whether genre evidence exists. Measured: a feature-only query scores an untagged candidate 1.00 and a tagged one 0.80; a genre-seeded query scores them 0.80 and 1.00. 0.8 is not an estimate, it IS the composition weight. Neither direction is a defect and neither is fixable by tuning a weight - it is what cosine length normalisation MEANS when a document carries a field the query does not, and cancelling it with a synthetic "unknown" bin for untagged tracks is rejected for the reason the core rejects everywhere else (it fabricates a genre direction). What it changes is the cutover: the corpus is ~98% genre-less, so a genre-seeded v2 query hands ~98% of the corpus a flat 0.8 handicap - the PR #135 starvation mechanism in a new hat - while a feature-only v2 query systematically buries the annotated slice v2 exists to exploit. **`DISCOVERY_FEATURE_ONLY_TARGET` and `DISCOVERY_MIN_COSINE` therefore stop being independent settings the moment `EMBEDDING_V2_READ` flips and must be swept TOGETHER** with `measureDiscoveryComposition.js`. Pinned as a test, and written into both ADR-0014's addendum and H13 next to the floor retune so it cannot be rediscovered after the flip. **THE BACKFILL DELIBERATELY DEVIATES from session 54's owed-list wording** ("computes the IDF table once via `idfStats.use()` and hands the same blob to every vector"), and the reason is recorded rather than left to be noticed: that shape would create a SECOND place that builds a stored vector, with its own copy of the `spotify:`/`youtube:` ToS gate, its own genre lookup and its own model tag - a second thing to drift, which is exactly what `tempo.js`'s one-home rule exists to prevent, and the repo already made this call once in `reembedCorpus.js`. `app/scripts/backfillEmbeddingV2.js` instead scans rows with no `vectorV2` and re-enqueues them onto EMBEDDING_BUILD, where the ONE writer writes, so a backfilled vector is byte-identical to an ingested one by construction. Resumability IS the filter (`{vectorV2:{$exists:false}}`) - a killed run resumes with no bookkeeping, a finished run scans to zero - and it REFUSES to start with the write flag off, because otherwise it would re-embed the whole corpus, write no v2 vector, and report a confident "done". Cost stated honestly: the worker resolves the IDF table through `idfStats.peek()` (6h cache), so a long run rebuilds it a few times instead of once - bounded by the maths (df moves O(1/N) on a near-static catalogue, and the genre block is L2-normalised per vector so a uniform weight shift divides straight back out) and idempotent to redo. **Also landed:** `TrackEmbedding` gains `vectorV2`/`dimV2`/`modelV2` as a second path on the SAME doc (so `recordingKey` stays unique and the cutover is an index+path switch with nothing to migrate), with NO defaults - "never built" must stay distinguishable from "built empty", because that distinction is what the backfill's resume filter reads. The adapter's v2 write is non-destructive in both directions: a v1-only write never `$unset`s a v2 vector, so the flag can be switched off and on again without destroying the backfill, and `dimV2` is derived from the vector rather than trusted from the caller. `getMany({version:'v2'})` OMITS a row with no v2 vector rather than falling back to v1 - a half-backfilled corpus degrades to "no embedding" (MMR already handles that with feature distance) and never to a mixed-space comparison. `fakeVectorIndex` models both spaces including the omit rule, because almost every discovery/pipeline suite runs on it and a fake that ignored `version` would green-light exactly the half-flip the real adapter fails on. `verifyProdRunbooks` gains Runbook 4: SKIPPED with the create instruction while the read flag is off (a deliberately absent index must never be a red check, or the operator learns to ignore the verifier), FAIL the moment the read flag is on and it is missing, FAIL either way for an index that EXISTS but is wrong. The ENABLE-flag spelling table moved to `utils/envFlag` so `SCORING_V2_SHADOW` and the two embedding flags cannot drift on what "off" means (`X=0` must never enable - W4-D05 in reverse); `shadowCompare` now calls it, behaviour unchanged. **COMPLIANCE (ADR-0011/0012):** the v2 path does not widen the containment by one row - the genre lookup runs over the docs that SURVIVED the `spotify:`/`youtube:` gate, so a restricted key is not even LOOKED UP for genres, and that is pinned. **TWO FIXES THIS SESSION THAT ARE NOT v2 WORK, both found by running rather than by reading, both recorded rather than folded in silently.** (1) `idfStats.peek()` is 6h-cached, but the call that MISSES that cache computes document frequencies over the whole mbid slice - and under a genre-seeded v2 query that scan would land on a user's generation UNBOUNDED. It is now wrapped in the same `withVectorBudget` every other query here uses (<=500ms, fallback null -> the genre block degrades to zero, i.e. an audio-only query, which is still a correct point in the space). Unreachable today (`DISCOVERY_FEATURE_ONLY_TARGET` defaults ON, so seedGenres is empty and peek is never called), which is exactly why it would have shipped. (2) **`tests/morningState.integration.test.js` went RED in the session's first full run and GREEN in isolation** - not a flake to shrug at, a real race: mongoose builds indexes ASYNCHRONOUSLY after connect, and that suite never awaited the build, so its 'one row per user per local day' case could beat its own unique index into existence and fail claiming the constraint was missing. Fixed at the root with `await MorningState.syncIndexes()` in `beforeAll` - the S1a posture (fix test hygiene properly, never quarantine), and the pattern the neighbouring `rewardEvent.integration.test.js` already had right. Checked every other mongodb-memory-server suite for the same latent race: `rewardEvent` is the only other one asserting a unique-index rejection and it already calls `syncIndexes()`, so this was one file, not a class. **EVIDENCE.** Suite **220 passed, 220 total / 1 todo, 3480 passed, 3481 total** green TWICE, exit 0, 0 `^FAIL` lines in every captured log (baseline 219/3429 from session 54: +1 suite, +52 tests = EXACTLY the new pin count, so no pre-existing test was lost). Lint 0 errors on every touched file. Secret + attribution scans of the diff clean. Zero-knowledge: this task touches no vitals at all, and the new log lines carry counts and error messages only - no userId, no track identity. **14 mutants applied, 14 caught** (unknown-version falls back to v2; adapter stores a null v2; getMany(v2) falls back to the v1 vector; queryNear switches index but not path; worker drops genres; worker looks up restricted keys; worker stores an abstaining null; v2 query sent to the v1 index; a null target reaches $vectorSearch; the fake ranks v2 against v1; MMR keeps reading v1; runbook 4 inverts its SKIP/FAIL; backfill runs with the write flag off; the ENABLE flag treats '0' as ON) - and the tree was verified clean afterwards. **ZERO re-pins:** all 12 existing embedding / discovery / vector / runbook / shadow suites (159 tests) pass untouched. **CI CHECKED AT CLOSE-OUT PER THE W4-D25 GATE, NOT ASSUMED, and reported exactly as observed rather than rounded up:** on the CODE head `b1cc26c` (every source and test change in this session) **ALL 10 CHECKS GREEN** (run 32539163719) - `Backend - lint & test`, `Mobile - jest`, `Mobile - Android compile check`, gitleaks, GitGuardian, frontend typecheck and both Vercel deployments. On the final head `cf9d87c` (which adds ONLY this STATE write and the untracking of Daniel's design brief - zero code) **9 of 10 green at close-out**, including `Backend - lint & test` at 2m21s; the tenth, `Mobile - Android compile check`, was still compiling when the session ended. ## HITL queue (for Daniel â€” numbered tutorials, decisions, portal actions)

- **H13 - PORTAL / PROD ACTION + DECISION: the v2 Atlas vector index, and the `DISCOVERY_MIN_COSINE` retune that must ride with it (W4-014).**
  Atlas `numDimensions` is IMMUTABLE per index, so v2 (135 dims: a 7-dim audio block + a 128-dim genre block)
  cannot reuse `track_embedding_index` (70 dims) - it needs a second index on a second path. That is a portal
  action an agent must never perform, so the whole of W4-014 ships dark behind `EMBEDDING_V2_WRITE` /
  `EMBEDDING_V2_READ`; with both unset the v1 path is byte-identical and nothing about serving changes.
  **Nothing is blocked on this today** - the pure core has no writer yet (the wiring half is owed), so the
  path is empty until that lands. Steps for Daniel, once the wiring half has actually started writing rows:
  (1) Atlas -> the `trackembeddings` collection -> Atlas Search -> Create Search Index -> **Vector Search**;
  (2) name it `track_embedding_index_v2`; (3) definition
  `{"fields":[{"type":"vector","path":"vectorV2","numDimensions":135,"similarity":"cosine"}]}`;
  (4) wait for status `READY`, then confirm with `db.trackembeddings.getSearchIndexes()`.
  Build it on a mostly-empty path rather than after a full backfill - the same "cheaper before it grows"
  reasoning H4 carried for the TTL index. Note H4's lesson applies here too: verify the built index in the
  Atlas UI, not only through a tool that may silently drop an option it does not model.
  **THE DECISION RIDING WITH IT, and it is the part that can silently go wrong:** `DISCOVERY_MIN_COSINE` is
  0.3, retuned in PR #135 against v1 geometry - where every corpus vector sits in the all-positive orthant and
  cosines cluster ~0.99 (prod-measured, `docs/plans/mbid-representation-recalibration-vs-genre-seam.md`), so the
  floor is very nearly inert. v2 is centred: cosine spans [-1,1] and genuinely discriminates, and the SAME 0.3
  means something entirely different against it. **Flipping `EMBEDDING_V2_READ` without re-tuning that floor
  would silently change what discovery admits** - the #135 starvation failure mode, from the other direction.
  The instrument already exists: `app/scripts/measureDiscoveryComposition.js` (read-only, the T4 harness from the
  required-reading plan) reports M_retrieval and M_served per archetype, so the retune is a measured sweep, not a
  guess. Recorded in ADR-0014 as a hard prerequisite of the cutover rather than a follow-up.
  **Open question for Daniel, not for an agent:** whether the v2 read cutover happens inside Wave 4 at all, or
  whether it ships dark and the composition sweep runs as its own piece of work after the wave closes. W4-014 is
  STRETCH tier and the mission already says the cutover is HITL-blocked, so shipping dark is the default.
  **UPDATE (session 55, the wiring half landed) - the path now WRITES, so step (1)-(4) above are live work
  rather than a forward-looking note.** With `EMBEDDING_V2_WRITE=true` the embedding worker dual-writes
  `vectorV2` on every job, and `app/scripts/backfillEmbeddingV2.js` re-enqueues the existing corpus through
  that same worker (resume filter: rows with no `vectorV2`; it REFUSES to run with the write flag off, so it
  cannot re-embed the corpus and quietly write nothing). Build the index BEFORE the backfill, per the
  cheaper-before-it-grows reasoning already above.
  **A SECOND PREREQUISITE, measured this session and easy to miss because it is invisible until the flip:**
  a v2 genre block is 0.6 of a unit vector when a track is tagged and EXACTLY ZERO when it is not, so the
  query and the candidate must AGREE about whether genre evidence exists. Measured (not estimated - 0.8 IS
  the composition weight): a feature-only query scores an untagged candidate 1.00 and a tagged one 0.80; a
  genre-seeded query scores them 0.80 and 1.00. The corpus is ~98% genre-less, so a genre-seeded v2 query
  hands ~98% of the corpus a flat 0.8 handicap - the PR #135 starvation mechanism in a new hat - while a
  feature-only v2 query systematically buries the annotated slice v2 exists to exploit. **Consequence:
  `DISCOVERY_FEATURE_ONLY_TARGET` and `DISCOVERY_MIN_COSINE` stop being independent settings the moment
  `EMBEDDING_V2_READ` flips, and must be swept TOGETHER with `measureDiscoveryComposition.js`, not one at a
  time.** Neither direction is a defect and neither is fixable by tuning a weight - it is what cosine length
  normalisation means when a document carries a field the query does not. Pinned as a test and written into
  ADR-0014's addendum so it cannot be rediscovered the hard way.
  **The verifier now checks it for you:** `node scripts/verifyProdRunbooks.js` gained Runbook 4 - the v2
  index. It reports SKIPPED (with the create instruction) while `EMBEDDING_V2_READ` is off, so a
  deliberately-absent index is never a red check; it turns FAIL the moment the read flag is on and the index
  is missing, and an index that EXISTS but has the wrong path/dims/similarity is a FAIL either way.

- **H4 â€” PORTAL / PROD ACTION: index builds for the new `vitalsamples` collection (W4-004).** The
  `VitalSample` model declares two indexes that Mongoose creates automatically in dev and test but that must
  be built deliberately in production, because a background index build on a live collection is a Pause &
  Guide action an agent must never perform: (a) the query index `{ userId: 1, metric: 1, recordedAt: -1 }`,
  which every baseline read depends on â€” without it the first user with a year of vitals turns a baseline
  refresh into a collection scan; and (b) the TTL index `{ recordedAt: 1 }` with
  `expireAfterSeconds = 7776000` (90 days, env-tunable via `VITAL_SAMPLE_RETENTION_DAYS`), which is what makes
  the retention promise in the privacy documentation true rather than aspirational. Steps for Daniel, once the
  wiring half has actually started writing rows: (1) Atlas â†’ Collections â†’ `vitalsamples`, or a `mongosh`
  session against `MONGO_URI`; (2) `db.vitalsamples.createIndex({ userId: 1, metric: 1, recordedAt: -1 },
  { background: true })`; (3) `db.vitalsamples.createIndex({ recordedAt: 1 }, { expireAfterSeconds: 7776000,
  background: true })`; (4) `db.vitalsamples.getIndexes()` to confirm both, and check that the TTL monitor has
  begun expiring rows older than 90 days on the next daily pass. Nothing is blocked on this today â€” the
  collection is empty until the wiring half lands, and correctness does not depend on the indexes, only
  performance and the retention guarantee do. Worth doing BEFORE the collection grows: building a TTL index on
  a large collection is far more expensive than building it on an empty one.

  **STILL OPEN - 2026-08-19 (manual maintenance session). No index was created, deliberately, and the reason matters.** This session was asked to build both indexes through the MongoDB MCP server after first proving the MCP target IS production. It could do neither, because **there is no MongoDB MCP server connected to this session at all** - a tool search for MongoDB/Atlas database tools returns nothing; the `mongodb:*` entries visible in the environment are documentation SKILLS, not a live server. So there was no connection to enumerate databases from, no server host to report, and nothing to compare against `MONGO_URI`. **What was verified locally instead (credentials never printed):** `backend/.env` sets `MONGO_URI` to an Atlas SRV target, user `Kokonada_Data`, host `cluster0.aowqhlx.mongodb.net` - and, importantly, **with no database path and no `MONGO_DB_NAME`**, which per `backend/app/config/db.js` resolves to the DEFAULT database `test`. `NODE_ENV=development`. `VITAL_SAMPLE_RETENTION_DAYS` is unset, so the model default of 90 days gives `expireAfterSeconds = 7776000` exactly. **Stated plainly: this session could NOT determine what production uses.** The deployed service supplies its own `MONGO_URI`/`MONGO_DB_NAME`, which is not readable from the dev box, so whether `cluster0.aowqhlx` is the production deployment - and if so under which database name - is unknown here. Building indexes on that guess is precisely the failure mode the HITL exists to prevent: it would close H4 on a false premise and leave production silently unindexed. **What was produced instead:** `scripts/atlas/h4-vitalsamples-indexes.js` - both `createIndex` calls with `background: true` and the TTL carrying `expireAfterSeconds: 7776000`, preceded by a `db.getName()` / `connectionStatus` pre-flight so the target is confirmed before anything is built, and followed by a read-back that asserts the TTL actually carries `expireAfterSeconds` (a TTL index created without that option is a plain index that silently expires nothing) plus the `collMod` recovery if it does not. Paste it into the Atlas UI or a `mongosh` session against a target confirmed to be production. **H4 stays OPEN until Daniel runs it and the read-back shows both indexes.**

  **RESOLVED 2026-08-20 (Daniel, via Atlas UI + a Claude Code session with a real MongoDB MCP connection).** Target confirmed as production first, not assumed: `cluster0.aowqhlx.mongodb.net` has exactly one application database (`test` - `admin`/`config`/`local` are MongoDB-internal), Daniel is the sole user account writing to it right now, and no other database exists to confuse it with - so `test` genuinely is production at this stage, not a dev artifact. The Claude Code session built the query index `userId_1_metric_1_recordedAt_-1` cleanly through the MongoDB MCP server and verified it real and server-side via `$indexStats`. **The TTL index needed a second pass and a human eye to catch it:** the MCP server's `create-index` tool schema only formally documents `type` + `keys` for a classic index, no `expireAfterSeconds` field - the session passed it anyway, but could not mechanically prove server-side whether it landed, and said so rather than claiming success on an unverified point. Daniel checked the Atlas UI's Indexes tab directly: the first attempt (`recordedAt_1_ttl`) showed as plain `REGULAR` with no `TTL` badge, confirming the option had been silently dropped exactly as feared - a TTL index built without it that would have expired nothing, forever, silently. Fix: dropped that index (empty, 0 uses, safe) and rebuilt it through Atlas's own index-creation UI, which has explicit native TTL support. Confirmed in the UI: `recordedAt_1_1`, `TTL` badge, `expireAfterSeconds: 7776000` (90 days), status `READY`. Both indexes are now real, server-side and correct: `userId_1_metric_1_recordedAt_-1` (compound query index, `READY`) and `recordedAt_1_1` (TTL, `expireAfterSeconds: 7776000`, `READY`). **H4 CLOSED.**

That is the real script against the real holder, not a stub (`-MaxIterations 0` guaranteed no session could launch even had the gate failed). A direct `Mutex.WaitOne(0)` probe on the same name returned `false` - held, not abandoned - and `Global\` creation succeeds **unelevated** on this box, so the privilege failure mode that would have silently voided the guard does not exist here. **(2) The two 17:00 processes were never Claude Code sessions.** `Win32_Process` shows PID 30736 (17:00:11) and PID 27424 (17:00:12) are both `--type=renderer` CHILDREN of PID 31104 = `C:\Program Files\WindowsApps\Claude_1.32885.1.0_x64__pzs8sxrjxfjjc\app\claude.exe`, alive since 03:24:14, whose other children are the ordinary Electron set (crashpad-handler, gpu-process, network/audio/video utility).   no merge conflicts, no force-pushes); `git status` shows only the known intentional item

  fix below â€” no leftover untracked files from the collision. `d1db088` (the ADR + tripwire
  commit) only touched 3 new/append-only files and never touched `WAVE4_STATE.md` or
  `run-mission.ps1`, confirming the three sessions did not actually corrupt each other's work â€”
  the halt worked exactly as designed, it just needed a human-reviewed close. Fix landed:
  `run-mission.ps1` now takes a named mutex (`Global\KokonadaWave4Loop`) before touching the
  tree and refuses to run a second instance (self-heals from an abandoned mutex if a prior loop
  was killed without releasing it), closing the root cause. `docs/plans/WAVE4_HALT` removal and
  the Task-Manager stray-process check are Daniel's â€” bridge tooling can't safely delete a
